5.
Time Foliations and the Carleman Estimate

Definition 5.1
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a FG-aAdS segment.
A smooth function
t:\mathscr{I}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is called a
global time
for
(\mathscr{M},g)
iff

•

The nonempty level sets of
t
are Cauchy hypersurfaces of
(\mathscr{I},\mathfrak{g})
.

•

There exists some
C>1
such that

22
22

22

Here
t
is extended in a
\rho
-independent manner to
\mathscr{M}
.

(5.1)

C^{-1}\leq-\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{D}^{\sharp}t,\mathsf{D}^{\sharp}t)\leq C

(in other words,
\mathsf{D}^{\sharp}t
is uniformly timelike).

For such a global time we additionally define the shorthands

23
23

23

Standard results in Lorentzian geometry
[
ONe83
,
Wal84
]
imply
\mathscr{I}
is diffeomorphic to
(t_{-},t_{+})\times\mathcal{S}
for some
(n-1)
-dimensional manifold
\mathcal{S}
, where
t
is the projection onto the
(t_{-},t_{+})
component.

(5.2)

t_{+}:=\sup_{\mathscr{I}}t\text{,}\qquad t_{-}:=\inf_{\mathscr{I}}t\text{.}

Our results will specifically pertain to spacetimes with the following class of boundaries:

Definition 5.2
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a FG-aAdS segment with a global time
t
. We say that
(\mathscr{M},g)
has
compact cross-sections
if and only if the sets

(5.3)

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}\cap\{t=\tau\}\text{,}\qquad t_{-}<\tau<t_{+}\text{,}

are compact.

In order to state our Carleman estimate, we require a coordinate-independent notion of the ‘size’ of a tensor field. To achieve this, we use a global time function to define a corresponding
Riemannian
metric:

Definition 5.3
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a FG-aAdS segment and
t
a global time. The
vertical Riemannian metric
associated with
(\mathsf{g},t)
is defined by

(5.4)

\mathsf{h}:=\mathsf{g}-\frac{2}{\mathsf{g}(\mathsf{D}^{\sharp}t,\mathsf{D}^{\sharp}t)}\,dt^{2}\text{.}

Furthermore, given a rank
(k,l)
vertical tensor field
\mathsf{A}
, we define its
\mathsf{h}
–norm
|\mathsf{A}|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}
in terms of coordinates on
\mathscr{I}
by

(5.5)

\displaystyle|\mathsf{A}|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}:=\Pi_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{h}_{a_{i}c_{i}}\cdot\Pi_{j=1}^{l}\mathsf{h}^{b_{j}d_{j}}\cdot\mathsf{A}^{a_{1}\cdots a_{k}}_{b_{1}\dots b_{l}}\mathsf{A}^{c_{1}\cdots c_{k}}_{d_{1}\dots d_{l}}\text{.}

We now define the weight that will feature in the Carleman estimate.

Definition 5.4
.

Suppose
\left(\mathscr{M},g\right)
is a FG-aAdS segment and
t
is a global time. With respect to some
0\leq b<c
, let

(5.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{b,c}:=(c^{2}-b^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\underbrace{\tan^{-1}\left[-b^{-1}(c^{2}-b^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]}_{\in[\frac{\pi}{2},\pi)}\text{,}

and suppose
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
is such that

(5.7)

\displaystyle t_{0}\pm\mathcal{T}_{b,c}\in(t_{-},t_{+})\text{.}

We then define the domain

(5.8)

\displaystyle\Omega:=\Omega_{t_{0},b,c}:=\{p\in\mathscr{M}\,|\,\left|t(p)-t_{0}\right|<\mathcal{T}_{b,c}\}\text{,}

and the function
f:=f_{t_{0},b,c}:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
by

(5.9)

\displaystyle f:=\frac{\rho}{\eta(t-t_{0})}\text{,}

where
\eta:=\eta_{b,c}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is given by

(5.10)

\displaystyle\eta(\tau)=e^{-b\left|\tau\right|}\cdot\sin\bigg[\underbrace{\tan^{-1}\left(-b^{-1}(c^{2}-b^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)}_{\in[\frac{\pi}{2},\pi)}-(c^{2}-b^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\left|\tau\right|\bigg]\text{.}

Finally, given some
f_{\ast}>0
, we define the region

(5.11)

\displaystyle\Omega(f_{\ast}):=\Omega\cap\{f<f_{\ast}\}\text{.}

The Carleman estimate of
[
MS20
, Theorem 5.11]
may now be stated in a form suitable for our purposes as follows.

Theorem 5.5
.

Suppose
\left(\mathscr{M},g\right)
is a vacuum FG-aAdS segment and
t
is a global time. Furthermore, suppose

•

\mathscr{I}
has compact cross-sections.

•

The null convexity criterion (
1.12
) holds on
\mathscr{I}
with associated constants
0\leq B<C
.

•

\Omega
,
f
are defined with respect to constants
B<b<c<C
for which (
5.7
) holds.

Fix
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
. Then, for any integers
k,l\geq 0
, there exist constants
C_{0}\geq 0
,
C_{1}>0
(both depending on
\mathsf{g}
,
t
,
B
,
C
,
b
,
c
,
k
,
l
) such that for all

•

\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying

(5.12)

\displaystyle 2\kappa\geq n-1+C_{0}\text{,}\qquad\kappa^{2}-(n-2)\kappa+\sigma-(n-1)-C_{0}\geq 0\text{,}

•

constants
f_{\ast},\lambda,p
satisfying

(5.13)

\displaystyle 0<f_{\ast}\ll 1\text{,}\qquad\lambda\gg\left|\kappa\right|+\left|\sigma\right|\text{,}\qquad 0<p<\frac{1}{2},

•

rank
(k,l)
vertical tensor fields
\mathsf{u}
on
\mathscr{M}
for which
\mathsf{u}
and
\bar{\nabla}\mathsf{u}
vanish on
\Omega\cap\{f=f_{\ast}\}
,

the following Carleman estimate holds:

(5.14)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega(f_{\ast})}

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda p^{-1}f^{p}}\cdot f^{n-2-p-2\kappa}\cdot\left|(\bar{\Box}+\sigma)\mathsf{u}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}dg

\displaystyle+C_{1}\lambda^{3}\limsup_{\rho^{\prime}\searrow 0}\int_{\Omega(f_{\ast})\cap\{\rho=\rho^{\prime}\}}\left|\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\left(\rho^{-\kappa}\mathsf{u}\right)\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+\left|\mathsf{D}_{\mathsf{D}^{\sharp}t}\left(\rho^{-\kappa}\mathsf{u}\right)\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+\left|\rho^{-\kappa-1}\mathsf{u}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}d\mathsf{g}|_{\rho^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\lambda\int_{\Omega(f_{\ast})}e^{-\lambda p^{-1}f^{p}}\cdot f^{n-2-2\kappa}\cdot\left(f\rho^{3}\left|\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{u}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+f\rho^{3}\left|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{u}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+f^{2p}\left|\mathsf{u}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}\right)dg\text{.}

Proof.

See
[
MS20
, Theorem 5.11]
. Note that our spacetime is automatically a
strongly
FG-aAdS segment (in the terminology used therein) due to our regularity assumptions and the fact that (
1.1
) is satisfied.
∎

6.
Local AdS Rigidity via the Boundary Data

6.1.
Preliminary Results

We begin by applying our Carleman estimate to prove unique continuation for the Weyl curvature given sufficiently fast vanishing along a sufficiently long timespan on the boundary.

Lemma 6.1
.

Fix
n>2
and
M_{0}\geq n+2
. Suppose
\left(\mathscr{M},g\right)
is an
(n+1)
–dimensional,
M_{0}
–regular vacuum FG-aAdS segment. Furthermore, suppose
\left(\mathscr{M},g\right)
has a global time
t
for which
\mathscr{I}
has compact cross-sections and the null convexity criterion holds on
\mathscr{I}
with associated constants
0\leq B<C
. Fix constants
B<b<c<C
and
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that (
5.7
) holds. Let
C_{0}
be the corresponding constant featuring in Theorem
5.5
and define

(6.1)

\displaystyle\kappa_{i}:=\max\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left[n-1+C_{0}\right]\text{,}\,\,\frac{1}{2}\left[n-2+\sqrt{n^{2}-4i(n-i)+4C_{0}}\right]\right\}\text{,}\qquad i=0,1,2\text{.}

Suppose that, on
\mathscr{I}\cap\Omega
,

(6.2)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{1}\left(\rho^{\kappa_{0}+2}\right)\text{,}\qquad\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}=\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(\rho^{\kappa_{0}+1}\right)\text{,}

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{1}\left(\rho^{\kappa_{1}+2}\right)\text{,}\qquad\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{W}^{1}=\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(\rho^{\kappa_{1}+1}\right)\text{,}

(6.4)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{1}\left(\rho^{\kappa_{2}+2}\right)\text{,}\qquad\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{W}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{0}\left(\rho^{\kappa_{2}+1}\right)\text{.}

Then the spacetime Weyl curvature identically vanishes on
\Omega\cap\{f<\frac{1}{2}f_{\ast}\}
.
24
24

24

f_{\ast}
is the corresponding constant in Theorem
5.5
.

Remark 6.2
.

The following bounds hold for each of the above-defined
\kappa_{i}
:

(6.5)

\displaystyle\kappa_{0}\geq n-1\text{,}\qquad\kappa_{1}\geq n-2\text{,}\qquad\kappa_{2}\geq n-3\text{.}

Proof.

Take some smooth cutoff function

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}:[0,f_{\ast}]\to[0,1]\text{,}\qquad\bar{\chi}(s)=\begin{cases}1\text{,}\qquad 0\leq s\leq\frac{1}{2}f_{\ast}\text{,}\\
0\text{,}\qquad\frac{3}{4}f_{\ast}\leq s\text{.}\end{cases}

Let
\chi:=\bar{\chi}\circ f
and, for convenience, define

(6.6)

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}^{0}:=\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{w}^{1}:=\mathsf{W}^{1}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{w}^{2}:=\mathsf{W}^{2}\text{.}

Let
\sigma_{i}:=i(n-i)
for
i=0,1,2
. Then

(6.7)

\displaystyle(\bar{\Box}_{g}+\sigma_{i})(\chi\cdot\mathsf{w}^{i})=

\displaystyle\chi(\bar{\Box}_{g}+\sigma_{i})\mathsf{w}^{i}+\chi^{\prime\prime}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}^{\alpha}f\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\alpha}f\,\mathsf{w}^{i}

\displaystyle\qquad+\chi^{\prime}\left(2\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\alpha}f\bar{\mathsf{D}}^{\alpha}\mathsf{w}^{i}+\bar{\Box}_{g}f\cdot\mathsf{w}^{i}\right)\text{,}

where
\chi^{\prime}
denotes the derivative of
\chi
with respect to
f
. Using that
\chi^{\prime},\chi^{\prime\prime}
are supported in
[\frac{1}{2}f_{\ast},\frac{3}{4}f_{\ast}]
and applying the relations derived in
[
MS20
, Proposition 5.7]
, one finds

(6.8)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{2}\left|(\bar{\Box}_{g}+\sigma_{i})(\chi\cdot\mathsf{w}^{i})\right|\lesssim\begin{cases}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\left(\left|(\bar{\Box}_{g}+\sigma_{i})\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+\rho^{2}f^{2}\left|\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}\right.\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\rho^{2}f^{4}\left|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+f^{2}\left|\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}\right)\text{,}\qquad\frac{1}{2}f_{\ast}\leq f\leq\frac{3}{4}f_{\ast}\\
\sum_{i=0}^{2}\left|(\bar{\Box}_{g}+\sigma_{i})\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}\text{,}\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad 0\leq f\leq\frac{1}{2}f_{\ast}\text{.}\end{cases}

(
4.51
), (
4.52
) and (
4.53
) in addition to the fact that
f\simeq 1
in
[\frac{1}{2}f_{\ast},\frac{3}{4}f_{\ast}]
implies that

(6.9)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{2}\left|(\bar{\Box}_{g}+\sigma_{i})(\chi\cdot\mathsf{w}^{i})\right|\lesssim\begin{cases}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\left(\rho^{2}\left|\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+\rho^{2}\left|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+\left|\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}\right)\text{,}\qquad\frac{1}{2}f_{\ast}\leq f\leq\frac{3}{4}f_{\ast}\\
\sum_{i=0}^{2}\left(\rho^{6}\left|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+\rho^{4}\left|\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}\right)\text{,}\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad 0\leq f\leq\frac{1}{2}f_{\ast}\text{.}\end{cases}

We define regions

(6.10)

\displaystyle\Omega_{i}:=

\displaystyle\Omega(f_{\ast})\cap\{f<\frac{1}{2}f_{\ast}\}

(6.11)

\displaystyle\Omega_{e}:=

\displaystyle\Omega(f_{\ast})\cap\{\frac{1}{2}f_{\ast}<f<\frac{3}{4}f_{\ast}\}

and sum the Carleman estimates (
5.14
), as applied to
\bar{\mathsf{w}^{i}}:=\chi\cdot\mathsf{w}^{i}
. The left hand side
L
of the sum of these Carleman estimates can be estimated by

(6.12)

\displaystyle L\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{2}\int_{\Omega_{e}}e^{-\lambda p^{-1}f^{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa_{i}-p}\left[\rho^{2}\left|\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+\rho^{2}\left|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+\left|\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}\right]dg

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=0}^{2}\int_{\Omega_{i}}e^{-\lambda p^{-1}f^{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa_{i}-p}\left[\rho^{6}\left|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+\rho^{4}\left|\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}\right]dg

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{C}\lambda^{3}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\limsup_{\rho_{\ast}\searrow 0}\int_{\Omega_{t_{0}}(f_{\ast})\cap\{\rho=\rho_{\ast}\}}[|\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{-\kappa_{i}}\bar{\mathsf{w}}^{i})|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+|\mathsf{D}(\rho^{-\kappa_{i}}\bar{\mathsf{w}}^{i})|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+|\rho^{-\kappa_{i}-1}\bar{\mathsf{w}}^{i}|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}]\,d\mathsf{g}|_{\rho_{\ast}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle L_{1}+L_{2}+L_{3}\text{.}

L
is bounded below by

(6.13)

\displaystyle L\gtrsim\lambda\sum_{i=0}^{2}\int_{\Omega_{i}}e^{-\lambda p^{-1}f^{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa_{i}}(f\rho^{3}|\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{i}|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+f\rho^{3}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{i}|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+f^{2p}|\mathsf{w}^{i}|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2})\,dg

L_{2}
can be absorbed by taking
\lambda
sufficiently large. By (
5.12
), the vanishing assumptions (
6.2
) and the fact that
|\partial_{\rho}\chi|+|\partial_{a}\chi|\lesssim\rho^{-1}
, we also have that
L_{3}\to 0
as
\rho_{\ast}\searrow 0
.

The
e^{-\lambda p^{-1}f^{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa_{i}}
factors can be bounded above in
\Omega_{e}
and bounded below in
\Omega_{i}
:

(6.14)

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda p^{-1}f^{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa_{i}}\begin{cases}\leq e^{-\lambda p^{-1}(\frac{f_{\ast}}{2})^{p}}\left(\frac{f_{\ast}}{2}\right)^{n-2-2\kappa_{i}}\text{,}\qquad\text{in $\Omega_{e}$}\\
\geq e^{-\lambda p^{-1}(\frac{f_{\ast}}{2})^{p}}\left(\frac{f_{\ast}}{2}\right)^{n-2-2\kappa_{i}}\text{,}\qquad\text{in $\Omega_{i}$.}\end{cases}

Hence, for large
\lambda
,

(6.15)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{2}\int_{\Omega_{e}}\left|\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+\rho^{2}\left|\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+\rho^{2}\left|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}dg\gtrsim\lambda\sum_{i=0}^{2}\int_{\Omega_{i}}f^{2p}\left|\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}dg

The left hand side of (
6.15
) is bounded above by

(6.16)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{2}\int_{\Omega_{e}}\left|\rho^{-\kappa_{i}-1}\mathsf{w}^{i}\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+\left|\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{-\kappa_{i}}\mathsf{w}^{i})\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}+\left|\mathsf{D}(\rho^{-\kappa_{i}}\mathsf{w}^{i})\right|_{\mathsf{h}}^{2}dg\text{,}

and so is finite by the vanishing assumptions (
6.2
). As a result, taking
\lambda\to\infty
in (
6.15
) yields
\mathsf{w}^{0},\mathsf{w}^{1},\mathsf{w}^{2}\equiv 0
(i.e.
\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0},\mathsf{W}^{1},\mathsf{W}^{2}\equiv 0
) on
\Omega\cap\{f<\frac{1}{2}f_{\ast}\}
. In other words, the full spacetime Weyl curvature
W\equiv 0
on
\Omega\cap\{f<\frac{1}{2}f_{\ast}\}
as required.
∎

Lemma 6.3
.

Fix
n>2
and
M_{0}\geq n+2
. Suppose
\left(\mathscr{M},g\right)
is an
(n+1)
–dimensional,
M_{0}
–regular vacuum FG-aAdS segment. If
\mathfrak{g}
is conformally flat then

(6.17)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{0}

\displaystyle=\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(n-2)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{0}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{r}_{0}\to^{M_{0}-n}0\text{,}

(6.18)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{1}

\displaystyle=\rho^{n-1}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(n-1)}+\rho^{n-1}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{1}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{r}_{1}\to^{M_{0}-n-1}0\text{,}

(6.19)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{2}

\displaystyle=\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(n-2)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{2}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{r}_{2}\to^{M_{0}-n}0\text{.}

Proof.

Recall the leading-order expressions for
\mathsf{W}^{0}
,
\mathsf{W}^{1}
and
\mathsf{W}^{2}
in Proposition
4.12
. If
n=3
then
\mathfrak{W}
identically vanishes and conformal flatness of
\mathfrak{g}
implies that
\mathfrak{C}
vanishes. Corollary
4.11
then yields (
6.17
)–(
6.19
) as required.

If
n>3
then conformal flatness of
\mathfrak{g}
implies that
\mathfrak{W}
vanishes, which in turn implies that
\mathfrak{C}
vanishes. Hence

(6.20)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(0)}=\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(1)}=\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(0)}=0\text{.}

If
n=4
then this implies

(6.21)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{0}

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}\log\rho\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(\star)}+\rho^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(2)}+\rho^{2}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{0}\text{,}

(6.22)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{1}

\displaystyle=\rho^{3}\log\rho\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(\star)}+\rho^{3}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(3)}+\rho^{3}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{1}\text{,}

(6.23)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{2}

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}\log\rho\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(\star)}+\rho^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(2)}+\rho^{2}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{2}\text{.}

Given (
6.23
), the left-hand side of the vertical Bianchi equation (
4.47
) reads

(6.24)

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\left(\rho^{-2}\cdot\mathsf{W}^{2}\right)=\rho\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(\star)}+o(\rho)\text{,}

where
o(\rho)
denotes a vertical tensor field
\mathsf{t}
for which
\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{t}\rightarrow^{0}0
. The factors of
\mathsf{g}^{-1}
and
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}
present in the right-hand side of (
4.47
) are
\mathscr{O}(1)
and
\mathscr{O}(\rho)
respectively; given (
6.21
) and (
6.22
), the right-hand side thus only contains
\mathscr{O}(\rho^{3}\log\rho)
terms. Collecting strictly order
\rho
terms in (
4.47
), one finds

(6.25)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(\star)}=0\text{,}

which yields (
6.19
) as required.

Given (
6.21
), the left-hand side of (
4.44
) reads

\displaystyle\rho\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{W}^{0}=\rho^{2}(1+2\log\rho)\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(\star)}+2\rho^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(2)}+o(\rho^{2})\text{.}

As above, the factors of
\mathsf{g}^{-1}
and
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}
present in the right-hand side of (
4.44
) are
\mathscr{O}(1)
and
\mathscr{O}(\rho)
respectively; given (
6.21
) and (
6.19
), the right-hand side thus only contains
\mathscr{O}(\rho^{2})
terms. Collecting strictly order
\rho^{2}\log\rho
terms in (
4.44
), one finds

(6.26)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(\star)}=0\text{,}

which yields (
6.17
) as required. Note that is was essential for us to derive (
6.19
)
before
completing this step; otherwise, the right-hand side of (
4.44
) would still contain strictly order
\rho^{2}\log\rho
terms.

Finally, given (
6.22
), the left-hand side of (
4.46
) reads

\displaystyle\rho\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\left(\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{W}^{1}\right)=\rho^{2}(1+2\log\rho)\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(\star)}+2\rho^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(3)}+o(\rho^{2})\text{.}

Once more, the factors of
\mathsf{g}^{-1}
and
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}
present in the right-hand side of (
4.46
) are
\mathscr{O}(1)
and
\mathscr{O}(\rho)
respectively. Given (
6.22
) and (
6.19
), the right-hand side thus only contains
\mathscr{O}(\rho^{2})
terms; collecting strictly order
\rho^{2}\log\rho
terms in (
4.46
), one finds

(6.27)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(\star)}=0\text{,}

which yields (
6.18
) as required. Again, it was crucial to derive (
6.19
) before completing this step to remove all order
\rho^{2}\log\rho
terms from the right-hand side of (
4.44
). This completes the proof of the
n=4
case.

For
n>4
, (
6.20
) yields

(6.28)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{0}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{n-3}{2}}\rho^{2k}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(2k)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(n-2)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{0}\text{,}&\text{$n$ odd,}\\
\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{n-4}{2}}\rho^{2k}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(2k)}+\rho^{n-2}\log\rho\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(\star)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(n-2)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{0}\text{,}&\text{$n$ even,}\end{cases}

(6.29)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{1}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{n-3}{2}}\rho^{2k+1}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(2k+1)}+\rho^{n-1}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(n-1)}+\rho^{n-1}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{1}\text{,}&\text{$n$ odd,}\\
\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{n-4}{2}}\rho^{2k+1}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(2k+1)}+\rho^{n-1}\log\rho\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(\star)}+\rho^{n-1}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(n-1)}+\rho^{n-1}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{1}\text{,}&\text{$n$ even,}\end{cases}

(6.30)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{2}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{n-3}{2}}\rho^{2k}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(2k)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(n-2)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{2}\text{,}&\text{$n$ odd,}\\
\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{n-4}{2}}\rho^{2k}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(2k)}+\rho^{n-2}\log\rho\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(\star)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(n-2)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{2}\text{,}&\text{$n$ even.}\end{cases}

Given (
6.30
), the left-hand side of the vertical Bianchi equation (
4.47
) reads

(6.31)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{n-3}{2}}(2k+2-n)\rho^{2k-1}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(2k)}+o(\rho^{n-2})\text{,}&\text{$n$ odd,}\\
\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{n-4}{2}}(2k+2-n)\rho^{2k-1}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(2k)}+\rho^{n-3}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(\star)}+o(\rho^{n-2})\text{,}&\text{$n$ even.}\end{cases}

Given (
6.28
) and (
6.29
), the right-hand side thus only contains
\mathscr{O}(\rho^{3})
terms. Collecting strictly order
\rho
terms in (
4.47
), one finds

(6.32)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(2)}=0\text{.}

In other words, for
n=5
we have (
6.19
) as required and, for
n>5
,

(6.33)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{2}=\begin{cases}\rho^{4}\log\rho\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(\star)}+\rho^{4}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(4)}+\rho^{4}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{2}\text{,}&\text{$n=6$,}\\
\sum_{k=2}^{\frac{n-3}{2}}\rho^{2k}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(2k)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(n-2)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{2}\text{,}&\text{$n>6$ odd,}\\
\sum_{k=2}^{\frac{n-4}{2}}\rho^{2k}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(2k)}+\rho^{n-2}\log\rho\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(\star)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(n-2)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{2}\text{,}&\text{$n>6$ even.}\end{cases}

Given (
6.28
), the left-hand side of (
4.44
) reads

(6.34)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{n-3}{2}}(2k)\rho^{2k}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(2k)}+(n-2)\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(n-2)}+o(\rho^{n-2})\text{,}\,\,&\text{$n$ odd,}\\
\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{n-4}{2}}(2k)\rho^{2k}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(2k)}+\rho^{n-2}\left[1+(n-2)\log\rho\right]\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(\star)}\\
\qquad+(n-2)\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(n-2)}+o(\rho^{n-2})\text{,}&\text{$n$ even.}\end{cases}

Given (
6.28
), (
6.29
) and (
6.33
) (or (
6.19
) if
n=5
), the right-hand side thus only contains

(6.35)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathscr{O}(\rho^{3})\,\,\text{terms if $n=5$,}\\
\mathscr{O}(\rho^{4}\log\rho)\,\,\text{terms if $n=6$,}\\
\mathscr{O}(\rho^{4})\,\,\text{terms if $n>6$.}\\
\end{cases}

Collecting strictly order
\rho^{2}
terms in (
4.44
), one hence finds

(6.36)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(2)}=0\text{.}

In other words, for
n=5
, we have (
6.17
) as required and, for
n>5
,

(6.37)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{0}=\begin{cases}\rho^{4}\log\rho\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(\star)}+\rho^{4}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(4)}+\rho^{4}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{0}\text{,}&\text{$n=6$,}\\
\sum_{k=2}^{\frac{n-3}{2}}\rho^{2k}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(2k)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(n-2)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{0}\text{,}&\text{$n>6$ odd,}\\
\sum_{k=2}^{\frac{n-4}{2}}\rho^{2k}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(2k)}+\rho^{n-2}\log\rho\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(\star)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(n-2)}+\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{0}\text{,}&\text{$n>6$ even.}\end{cases}

Given (
6.29
), the left-hand side of (
4.46
) reads

(6.38)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{n-3}{2}}(2k)\rho^{2k}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(2k+1)}+(n-2)\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(n-1)}+o(\rho^{n-2})\text{,}\,\,&\text{$n$ odd,}\\
\sum_{k=1}^{\frac{n-4}{2}}(2k)\rho^{2k}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(2k+1)}+\rho^{n-2}\left[1+(n-2)\log\rho\right]\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(\star)}\\
\qquad+(n-2)\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(n-1)}+o(\rho^{n-2})\text{,}&\text{$n$ even.}\end{cases}

Given (
6.29
) and (
6.33
) (or (
6.19
) if
n=5
), the right-hand side thus only contains

(6.39)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathscr{O}(\rho^{3})\,\,\text{terms if $n=5$,}\\
\mathscr{O}(\rho^{4}\log\rho)\,\,\text{terms if $n=6$,}\\
\mathscr{O}(\rho^{4})\,\,\text{terms if $n>6$.}\\
\end{cases}

Collecting strictly order
\rho^{2}
terms in (
4.46
), one hence finds

(6.40)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(3)}=0\text{.}

In other words, for
n=5
, we have (
6.18
) as required and, for
n>5
,

(6.41)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{1}=\begin{cases}\rho^{5}\log\rho\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(\star)}+\rho^{5}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(5)}+\rho^{5}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{1}\text{,}&\text{$n=6$,}\\
\sum_{k=2}^{\frac{n-3}{2}}\rho^{2k+1}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(2k+1)}+\rho^{n-1}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(n-1)}+\rho^{n-1}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{1}\text{,}&\text{$n>6$ odd,}\\
\sum_{k=2}^{\frac{n-4}{2}}\rho^{2k+1}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(2k+1)}+\rho^{n-1}\log\rho\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(\star)}+\rho^{n-1}\cdot\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(n-1)}+\rho^{n-1}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{1}\text{,}&\text{$n>6$ even.}\end{cases}

This completes the proof for the
n=5
case. For
n>5
, one may now iterate this process to derive vanishing of successively higher-order coefficients; at the
k\textsuperscript{th}
iteration, substitute the updated expansions of
\mathsf{W}^{0}
,
\mathsf{W}^{1}
and
\mathsf{W}^{2}
into (
4.47
), then (
4.44
) and (
4.46
), collecting order
\rho^{2k+1}
,
\rho^{2k+2}
and
\rho^{2k+2}
terms respectively (or order
\rho^{2k+1}
,
\rho^{2k+2}\log\rho
and
\rho^{2k+1}\log\rho
if dealing with
\log
coefficients).

Note that this process cannot be continued to derive vanishing of
\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(n-2)}
(and hence
\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(n-2)}
,
\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(n-1)}
too), since this coefficient is always eliminated upon substitution of (
6.19
) into (
4.47
).
∎

Corollary 6.4
.

Fix
n>2
and
M_{0}\geq n+2
. Suppose
\left(\mathscr{M},g\right)
is an
(n+1)
–dimensional,
M_{0}
–regular vacuum FG-aAdS segment. If
\mathfrak{g}
is conformally flat then

(6.42)

\displaystyle\mathsf{g}=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{g}-\rho^{2}\,\mathfrak{p}+\rho^{n}\,\mathfrak{g}^{(n)}+\rho^{n}\,\mathsf{r}\text{,}\qquad&n\leq 4\text{,}\\
\mathfrak{g}-\rho^{2}\,\mathfrak{p}+\frac{1}{4}\rho^{4}\,\mathfrak{g}^{-1}\,\mathfrak{p}\,\mathfrak{p}+\rho^{n}\,\mathfrak{g}^{(n)}+\rho^{n}\,\mathsf{r}\text{,}\qquad&n>4\text{,}\end{cases}

where the remainder
\mathsf{r}
satisfies

(6.43)

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}\to^{M_{0}-n}\,\,0\text{.}

Furthermore, there exist tensor fields
\mathfrak{g}_{(k)}
on
\mathscr{I}
such that

(6.44)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}^{k}\mathsf{g}^{-1}\rightarrow^{M_{0}-k}\,k!\,\mathfrak{g}_{(k)}\text{,}\qquad 0\leq k<n\text{,}

where
\mathfrak{g}_{(k)}
vanishes for
k
odd and, for
0<2l<n
,

(6.45)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{(2l)}^{ab}

\displaystyle=\frac{l+1}{2^{l}}\cdot\left(\mathfrak{g}^{ac_{1}}\mathfrak{g}^{c_{2}c_{3}}\dots\mathfrak{g}^{c_{2l}b}\right)\cdot\left(\mathfrak{p}_{c_{1}c_{2}}\mathfrak{p}_{c_{3}c_{4}}\dots\mathfrak{p}_{c_{2l-1}c_{2l}}\right)\text{,}

(6.46)

\displaystyle=\frac{l+1}{2l}\,\mathfrak{g}^{ac}\,\mathfrak{p}_{cd}\,\mathfrak{g}^{db}_{(2l-2)}\text{.}

Proof.

Theorem
4.3
gave the near–boundary expansion of
\mathsf{g}
and established, furthermore, that
\mathfrak{g}^{(2)}=-\mathfrak{p}
. To prove (
6.42
), we must therefore show that

(1)

When
n>4
,
\mathfrak{g}^{(4)}=\frac{1}{4}\,\mathfrak{g}^{-1}\,\mathfrak{p}\,\mathfrak{p}
.

(2)

When
n>6
,
\mathfrak{g}^{(k)}=0
for
6\leq k<n
.

(3)

When
n\geq 4
,
\mathfrak{g}^{(\star)}=0
.

We address each of these statements in turn:

(1) This follows from (
4.35
) and (
6.19
) (i.e.
\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(2)}=0
).

(2) We proceed via induction. (
4.36
) combined with (
6.19
) (i.e.
\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(4)}=0
) provides the base case. Fix
6\leq 2K<n-2
and assume that
\mathfrak{g}^{(j)}=0
for all
6\leq j\leq 2K
, i.e.

(6.47)

\displaystyle\mathsf{g}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{g}-\rho^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{p}+\frac{1}{4}\rho^{4}\cdot\mathfrak{g}^{-1}\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{p}

\displaystyle\qquad+\begin{cases}\sum_{k=K+1}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\rho^{2k}\,\mathfrak{g}^{(2k)}+\rho^{n}\,\mathfrak{g}^{(n)}+\rho^{n}\,\mathsf{r}\text{,}\qquad&\text{$n$ odd}\text{,}\\
\sum_{k=K+1}^{\frac{n-2}{2}}\rho^{2k}\,\mathfrak{g}^{(2k)}+\rho^{n}\log\rho\,\mathfrak{g}^{(\star)}+\rho^{n}\,\mathfrak{g}^{(n)}+\rho^{n}\,\mathsf{r}\text{,}\qquad&\text{$n$ even}\text{.}\end{cases}

Substituting this into the relation

(6.48)

\displaystyle\mathsf{g}_{ab}\cdot\mathsf{g}^{bc}=\delta_{a}^{c}\text{,}

and applying the inductive assumption to match coefficients order-by-order, one finds that

(6.49)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathfrak{g}_{(0)}^{ab}=\mathfrak{g}^{ab}\\
\mathfrak{g}_{(2)}^{ab}=\mathfrak{g}^{ac}\mathfrak{g}^{bd}\mathfrak{p}_{cd}\\
\mathfrak{g}_{ab}\cdot\mathfrak{g}_{(2l)}^{bc}-\mathfrak{p}_{ab}\cdot\mathfrak{g}_{(2l-2)}^{bc}+\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{g}^{de}\cdot\mathfrak{p}_{ad}\cdot\mathfrak{p}_{be}\cdot\mathfrak{g}_{(2l-4)}^{bc}=0\text{,}\qquad 4\leq 2l\leq 2K\text{,}\end{cases}

where
\mathfrak{g}^{ab}:=\left(\mathfrak{g}^{-1}\right)^{ab}
. One may straightforwardly verify that this is satisfied by the ansatz (
6.45
), which in turn satisfies the recurrence relation (
6.46
).

Substituting (
6.19
) into the left and (
6.47
) into the right-hand side of (
4.21
) and collecting strictly order
\rho^{2K}
terms, one finds

(6.50)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=-2K(K+1)\mathfrak{g}^{(2K+2)}_{ab}+\mathfrak{g}_{(2K-2)}^{cd}\mathfrak{p}_{ac}\mathfrak{p}_{bd}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{g}_{(2K-4)}^{cd}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{ac}\mathfrak{g}^{ef}\mathfrak{p}_{be}\mathfrak{p}_{df}+\mathfrak{g}^{ef}\mathfrak{p}_{ae}\mathfrak{p}_{cf}\mathfrak{p}_{bd}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{g}_{(2K-6)}^{cd}\mathfrak{g}^{ef}\mathfrak{p}_{ae}\mathfrak{p}_{cf}\mathfrak{g}^{gh}\mathfrak{p}_{bg}\mathfrak{p}_{dh}\text{.}

Applying the relations (
6.46
), one finds that all but the first of the above terms cancel. Hence

(6.51)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{(2K+2)}=0\text{,}

which closes the inductive argument.

(3) Substituting (
6.19
) and the properties derived above in parts (1) and (2) into the left-hand side of (
4.21
), (
4.5
) into the right-hand side of (
4.21
) and collecting strictly order
\rho^{n-2}\log\rho
terms, one finds

(6.52)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{(\star)}=0\text{,}

as required.
∎

Proposition 6.5
.

Fix
n>2
and
M_{0}\geq n+2
. Suppose
(\mathscr{M},g)
is an
(n+1)
–dimensional,
M_{0}
–regular vacuum FG-aAdS segment. Then there exist vertical tensor fields
\mathsf{r}_{0}
,
\mathsf{r}_{1}
and
\mathsf{r}_{2}
such that

(6.53)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{0}

\displaystyle=\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{0}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{r}_{0}\to^{M_{0}-n}0\text{,}

(6.54)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{1}

\displaystyle=\rho^{n-1}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{1}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{r}_{1}\to^{M_{0}-n-1}0\text{,}

(6.55)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{2}

\displaystyle=\rho^{n-2}\cdot\mathsf{r}_{2}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{r}_{2}\to^{M_{0}-n}0\text{.}

if and only if
\left(\mathscr{M},g\right)
has boundary data
\left(\mathfrak{g}^{(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{(n)}\right)
for which

(6.56)

\mathfrak{g}^{(0)}
is conformally flat, and

(6.57)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{(n)}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{4}\,\mathfrak{g}^{-1}\cdot\mathfrak{p}\cdot\mathfrak{p}\text{,}\qquad&n=4\text{,}\\
0\text{,}\qquad&n\neq 4\text{,}\end{cases}

where
\mathfrak{p}
is the
\mathfrak{g}^{(0)}=\mathfrak{g}
–Schouten tensor.

Proof.

We begin by proving the reverse direction of this statement. Given (
6.56
), we obtain a simplified expansion of
\mathsf{g}
via Corollary
6.4
. Substituting this along with (
6.19
) into (
4.21
) and collecting order
\rho^{n-2}
terms, we find

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{W}_{2}}^{(n-2)}_{ab}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}n(n-2)\mathfrak{g}^{(n)}_{ab}

(6.58)

\displaystyle\qquad+\begin{cases}\makebox[234.0065pt][l]{$0\text{,}$}\text{$n$ odd,}\\
\makebox[234.0065pt][l]{$\mathfrak{p}_{ac}\mathfrak{p}_{bd}\mathfrak{g}^{cd}\text{,}$}\text{$n=4$,}\\
\makebox[234.0065pt][l]{$\mathfrak{p}_{ac}\mathfrak{p}_{bd}\mathfrak{g}_{(2)}^{cd}-\mathfrak{g}^{ef}\mathfrak{p}_{ac}\mathfrak{p}_{be}\mathfrak{p}_{df}\mathfrak{g}^{cd}\text{,}$}\text{$n=6$,}\\
\mathfrak{p}_{ac}\mathfrak{p}_{bd}\mathfrak{g}_{(n-4)}^{cd}-\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{g}^{ef}\left(\mathfrak{p}_{ac}\mathfrak{p}_{be}\mathfrak{p}_{df}+\mathfrak{p}_{ae}\mathfrak{p}_{cf}\mathfrak{p}_{bd}\right)\mathfrak{g}_{(n-6)}^{cd}\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{g}^{ef}\mathfrak{g}^{gh}\mathfrak{p}_{ae}\mathfrak{p}_{cf}\mathfrak{p}_{bg}\mathfrak{p}_{dh}\mathfrak{g}_{(n-8)}^{cd}\text{,}\,\,\,\,n\geq 8\text{.}\end{cases}

The relations (
6.46
) result in each of the
n\geq 6
terms in the brace vanishing identically. The above hence reduces to

(6.59)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{W}_{2}}^{(n-2)}_{ab}=\begin{cases}-\frac{1}{2}n(n-2)\mathfrak{g}^{(n)}_{ab}\text{,}\qquad&n\neq 4\text{,}\\
-4\mathfrak{g}^{(4)}_{ab}+\mathfrak{g}^{cd}\,\mathfrak{p}_{ac}\,\mathfrak{p}_{bd}\text{,}\qquad&n=4\text{,}\end{cases}

from which we see that (
6.57
) implies

(6.60)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(n-2)}=0\text{.}

We have thus used this condition to overcome the barrier to the iterative process identified in the proof of Lemma
6.3
; substituting the updated expansions (
4.28
), (
4.29
) and (
4.30
) into the vertical Bianchi equation (
4.44
) and collecting strictly order
\rho^{n-2}
terms, one finds

(6.61)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{0}^{(n-2)}=0\text{.}

Finally, substituting the updated expansions (
4.28
), (
4.29
) and (
4.30
) into the vertical Bianchi equation (
4.46
) and collecting strictly order
\rho^{n-2}
terms, one finds

(6.62)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{1}^{(n-1)}=0\text{.}

This yields (
6.53
), (
6.54
) and (
6.55
) as required.

For the forward direction, recall the leading-order expressions for
\mathsf{W}^{0}
and
\mathsf{W}^{1}
found Proposition
4.12
. If
n=3
then the vanishing of
{\mathfrak{W}_{1}}^{(1)}=\mathfrak{C}
guaranteed by (
6.54
) implies that
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{g}^{(0)}
is conformally flat. If
n>3
then the vanishing of
{\mathfrak{W}_{0}}^{(0)}=\mathfrak{W}
guaranteed by (
6.53
) implies that
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{g}^{(0)}
is conformally flat.

We hence have (
6.56
) and, as above, can apply Corollary
6.4
to substitute the simplified expansion of
\mathsf{g}
into (
4.21
) to obtain (
6.59
). The vanishing of
\mathfrak{W}_{2}^{(n-2)}
guaranteed by (
6.55
) thus implies the condition (
6.57
) as required.
∎

6.2.
The Main Result

Theorem 6.6
.

Fix
n>2
.
25
25

25

If
n=2
, the Weyl curvature identically vanishes. Hence, in this case, every vacuum FG-aAdS segment is locally isometric to pure AdS and the result in Theorem
6.6
is trivial.
Suppose
(\mathscr{M},g)
is an
(n+1)
–dimensional vacuum FG-aAdS segment with a global time
t
for which:

•

\mathscr{I}
has compact cross-sections.

•

The null convexity criterion holds on
\mathscr{I}
with associated constants
0\leq B<C
.

Fix constants
B<b<c<C
and
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that (
5.7
) holds. There exist constants
M_{0}\geq n+2
and
f_{\ast}>0
such that if
\left(\mathscr{M},g\right)
is
M_{0}
–regular then it is locally isometric to pure AdS on
\Omega(f_{\ast})
if and only if
\left(\mathscr{M},g\right)
has boundary data
\left(\mathfrak{g}^{(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{(n)}\right)
for which the following hold on
\mathscr{I}\cap\Omega
:

(6.63)

\mathfrak{g}^{(0)}
is conformally flat, and

(6.64)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{(n)}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{4}\,\mathfrak{g}^{-1}\cdot\mathfrak{p}\cdot\mathfrak{p}\text{,}\qquad&n=4\text{,}\\
0\text{,}\qquad&n\neq 4\text{,}\end{cases}

where
\mathfrak{p}
is the
\mathfrak{g}
–Schouten tensor.

Proof.

Solutions of (
1.1
) are locally isometric to AdS if and only if
26
26

26

See, for example,
[
Car04
]
.

(6.65)

\displaystyle R_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}=-g_{\alpha\gamma}g_{\beta\delta}+g_{\alpha\delta}g_{\beta\gamma}\text{,}

or, equivalently,

(6.66)

\displaystyle W_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}=0\text{.}

Vanishing of the spacetime Weyl curvature implies vanishing of
\mathsf{W}^{0}
,
\mathsf{W}^{1}
and
\mathsf{W}^{2}
to arbitrarily high order at
\mathscr{I}
. By Proposition
6.5
, the conditions (
6.63
) and (
6.64
) follow.

For the other direction, let us write
o_{K}(\rho^{L})
to denote a vertical tensor field
\mathsf{t}
for which

(6.67)

\displaystyle\rho^{-L}\cdot\mathsf{t}\rightarrow^{K}\,0\text{.}

In terms of this notation, (
6.63
) and (
6.64
) imply

(6.68)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{0}=o_{M_{0}-n}(\rho^{n-2})\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{W}^{1}=o_{M_{0}-n-1}(\rho^{n-1})\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{W}^{2}=o_{M_{0}-n}(\rho^{n-2})\text{,}

by Proposition
6.5
.

Substituting (
6.68
) into (
4.47
), one has

(6.69)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\left(\rho^{-(n-2)}\mathsf{W}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\rho^{-(n-2)}\cdot o_{M_{0}-n-2}(\rho^{n-1})+\rho^{-(n-2)}\cdot o_{M_{0}-n}(\rho^{n-1})

\displaystyle=o_{M_{0}-n-2}(\rho)\text{,}

where a degree of vertical regularity has been lost due to the vertical derivative present in the right-hand side of (
4.47
). Given (
6.68
),
\rho^{-(n-2)}\mathsf{W}^{2}
has vanishing boundary limit so we may integrate (
6.69
) from the boundary to deduce

(6.70)

\displaystyle\rho^{-(n-2)}\cdot\mathsf{W}^{2}=o_{M_{0}-n-2}(\rho^{2})\implies\mathsf{W}^{2}=o_{M_{0}-n-2}(\rho^{n})\text{.}

Substituting (
6.68
) and (
6.70
) into (
4.44
), one has

(6.71)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{W}^{0}

\displaystyle=o_{M_{0}-n-2}(\rho^{n-1})+\rho^{-1}\cdot o_{M_{0}-n-2}(\rho^{n})+\rho^{-1}\cdot o_{M_{0}-n}(\rho^{n})

\displaystyle=o_{M_{0}-n-2}(\rho^{n-1})\text{.}

Integrating this from the boundary, we deduce
27
27

27

Note that if we hadn’t first derived (
6.70
), then one would only have
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{W}^{0}=o_{M_{0}-n-2}(\rho^{n-3})
and we would not have been able to obtain this improvement via integration.

(6.72)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{0}=o_{M_{0}-n-2}(\rho^{n})\text{.}

Substituting (
6.68
) and (
6.70
) into (
4.46
), one has

(6.73)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\left(\rho^{-1}\mathsf{W}^{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\rho^{-1}\cdot o_{M_{0}-n-3}(\rho^{n})+\rho^{-1}\cdot o_{M_{0}-n-1}(\rho^{n})

\displaystyle=o_{M_{0}-n-3}(\rho^{n-1})\text{.}

Again, a degree of vertical regularity has been lost due to the vertical derivative present in the right-hand side of (
4.46
). Integrating from the boundary, we deduce
28
28

28

Again, it was crucial to derive (
6.70
) first.

(6.74)

\displaystyle\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathsf{W}^{1}=o_{M_{0}-n-3}(\rho^{n})\implies\mathsf{W}^{1}=o_{M_{0}-n-3}(\rho^{n+1})\text{.}

Iterating the above process (that is, integrating (
4.47
) followed by (
4.44
) and (
4.46
) using the improved rates (
6.70
), (
6.72
) and (
6.74
))
i
times, we deduce

(6.75)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{0}=o_{M_{0}-n-2i}(\rho^{n-2+2i})\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{W}^{1}=o_{M_{0}-n-1-2i}(\rho^{n-1+2i})\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{W}^{2}=o_{M_{0}-n-2i}(\rho^{n-2+2i})\text{.}

At each iteration, additional orders of vanishing are exchanged for degrees of vertical regularity. By Lemma
6.1
, for unique continuation to hold it will suffice for
n-2+2i\geq\kappa_{\text{max}}+2
, where

(6.76)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\text{max}}:=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}\left(n-2+\sqrt{n^{2}+4C_{0}}\right)\text{,}\qquad&C_{0}\leq n+1\text{,}\\
\frac{1}{2}\left(n-1+C_{0}\right)\text{,}\qquad&C_{0}>n+1\text{.}\end{cases}

In other words, we must iterate
i=\left\lceil{\frac{1}{2}\left(\kappa_{\text{max}}-n+4\right)}\right\rceil
times. If we choose
M_{0}=\left\lceil{\kappa_{\text{max}}}\right\rceil+6
then this yields

(6.77)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{0}=o_{2}(\rho^{\kappa_{\text{max}}+2})\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{W}^{1}=o_{1}(\rho^{\kappa_{\text{max}}+3})\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{W}^{2}=o_{2}(\rho^{\kappa_{\text{max}}+2})\text{,}

which in turn implies (
6.2
), (
6.3
) and (
6.4
) as required.
∎

The following Proposition demonstrates that, for the result of Theorem
6.6
to hold, it is only necessary for the relevant boundary data conditions to hold in
one
Fefferman-Graham coordinate system.

Proposition 6.7
.

Fix
n>2
and
M_{0}\geq n+2
. Suppose
(\mathscr{M},g)
is an
(n+1)
–dimensional,
M_{0}
–regular vacuum FG-aAdS segment. The conditions (
6.63
) and (
6.64
) are invariant under coordinate transformations
\left(\rho,x\right)\to\left(\tilde{\rho},\tilde{x}\right)
of the form

(6.78)

\displaystyle\rho

\displaystyle=\tilde{\rho}\cdot e^{-\sigma(\tilde{x})}+\tilde{\rho}^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{(2)}(\tilde{x})+\tilde{\rho}^{3}\cdot\mathfrak{a}^{(3)}(\tilde{x})+\tilde{\rho}^{3}\cdot\mathsf{s}\text{,}

(6.79)

\displaystyle x^{b}

\displaystyle=\tilde{x}^{b}+\tilde{\rho}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{(1)}^{b}(\tilde{x})+\tilde{\rho}^{2}\cdot\mathfrak{a}_{(2)}^{b}(\tilde{x})+\tilde{\rho}^{2}\cdot\mathsf{t}^{b}\text{,}

where
\sigma(\tilde{x})
is
C^{M_{0}+1}
on
\mathscr{I}
and
\mathsf{s}
and
\mathsf{t}
are vertical (with respect to
\tilde{\rho}
) tensor fields satisfying

(6.80)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}\to^{M_{0}-1}\,0\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{t}\to^{M_{0}-1}\,0\text{,}

for which the Fefferman-Graham gauge (
1.6
) is preserved, i.e. for which there exists some vertical (with respect to
\tilde{\rho}
) tensor field
\tilde{\mathsf{g}}
such that

(6.81)

\displaystyle g=\tilde{\rho}^{-2}\left[d\tilde{\rho}^{2}+\tilde{\mathsf{g}}_{ab}\,d\tilde{x}^{a}d\tilde{x}^{b}\right]\text{.}

Proof.

As in
[
Ske01
]
, one may uniquely determine the coefficients
\mathfrak{a}^{(k)}
and
\mathfrak{a}_{(k)}^{b}
order-by-order. At leading-order one finds

(6.82)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=0\text{,}\qquad\mathfrak{a}^{(3)}=-\frac{1}{4}e^{-3\sigma}\mathfrak{g}^{bc}\mathfrak{D}_{b}\sigma\,\mathfrak{D}_{c}\sigma\text{,}

(6.83)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{(1)}^{b}

\displaystyle=0\text{,}\qquad\mathfrak{a}_{(2)}^{b}=\frac{1}{2}e^{-2\sigma}\mathfrak{g}^{bc}\mathfrak{D}_{c}\sigma\text{,}

One may use this to compute

(6.84)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{\rho}}=\frac{\partial\rho}{\partial\tilde{\rho}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\rho}+\frac{\partial x^{b}}{\partial\tilde{\rho}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{b}}\text{,}\qquad\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{x}^{a}}=\frac{\partial\rho}{\partial\tilde{x}^{a}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\rho}+\frac{\partial x^{b}}{\partial\tilde{x}^{a}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{b}}\text{,}

and thus derive (to leading-order) the transformations of the vertical components of the Weyl curvature under the above change of coordinates. For example, one finds

(6.85)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\rho}{\partial\tilde{x}^{a}}=\mathscr{O}_{M_{0}-2}(\rho)_{a}\text{,}\qquad\frac{\partial x^{b}}{\partial\tilde{x}^{a}}=\delta^{b}_{a}+\mathscr{O}_{M_{0}-2}(\rho^{2})^{b}_{a}\text{,}

which implies that

(6.86)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathsf{W}}^{0}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}\tilde{c}\tilde{d}}

\displaystyle=\left(\rho e^{\sigma}+\mathscr{O}_{M_{0}-2}(\rho^{2})\right)^{2}\cdot W\left(\mathscr{O}_{M_{0}-2}(\rho)_{a}\frac{\partial}{\partial\rho}+\left(1+\mathscr{O}_{M_{0}-2}(\rho^{2})\right)\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{a}},\dots\right)

\displaystyle=e^{2\sigma}\mathsf{W}^{0}_{abcd}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-2}(\rho^{2};\mathsf{W}^{0})_{abcd}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-2}(\rho;\mathsf{W}^{1})_{abcd}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-2}(\rho^{2};\mathsf{W}^{2})_{abcd}\text{.}

Similarly, one finds

(6.87)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathsf{W}}^{1}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}\tilde{c}}

\displaystyle=e^{\sigma}\mathsf{W}^{1}_{abc}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-2}(\rho;\mathsf{W}^{0})_{abc}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-2}(\rho^{2};\mathsf{W}^{1})_{abc}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-2}(\rho;\mathsf{W}^{2})_{abc}\text{,}

(6.88)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathsf{W}}^{2}_{\tilde{a}\tilde{b}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{W}^{2}_{ab}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-2}(\rho^{2};\mathsf{W}^{0})_{ab}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-2}(\rho;\mathsf{W}^{1})_{ab}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-2}(\rho^{2};\mathsf{W}^{2})_{ab}\text{.}

Applying Proposition
6.5
, the conditions (
6.63
) and (
6.64
) imply (
6.53
), (
6.54
) and (
6.55
). By the above, we may hence deduce the existence of vertical tensor fields
\tilde{\mathsf{r}}_{0}
,
\tilde{\mathsf{r}}_{1}
and
\tilde{\mathsf{r}}_{2}
for which

(6.89)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathsf{W}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\rho}^{n-2}\cdot\tilde{\mathsf{r}}_{0}\text{,}\qquad\tilde{\mathsf{r}}_{0}\to^{M_{0}-n}0\text{,}

(6.90)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathsf{W}}^{1}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\rho}^{n-1}\cdot\tilde{\mathsf{r}}_{1}\text{,}\qquad\tilde{\mathsf{r}}_{1}\to^{M_{0}-n-1}0\text{,}

(6.91)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathsf{W}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\rho}^{n-2}\cdot\tilde{\mathsf{r}}_{2}\text{,}\qquad\tilde{\mathsf{r}}_{2}\to^{M_{0}-n}0\text{.}

Since Proposition
6.5
also applies with respect to the new coordinate system, we may apply it to conclude that

(6.92)

\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{(0)}
is conformally flat, and

(6.93)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{(n)}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{4}\,\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}\text{,}\qquad&n=4\text{,}\\
0\text{,}\qquad&n\neq 4\text{,}\end{cases}

as required.
∎

Appendix A
Proofs of Conversion Formulae

Given a mixed tensor field
\mathbf{A}
of rank
(\kappa,\lambda;k,l)
, Propositions
3.2
and
3.4
give the following formula for the mixed derivative in terms of
\varphi
- and
\varphi
-coordinates:

(A.1)

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}_{\gamma}\mathbf{A}^{\bar{\alpha}}{}_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\gamma}(\mathbf{A}^{\bar{\alpha}}{}_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}})+\sum_{i=1}^{\kappa}\Gamma^{\alpha_{i}}_{\gamma\delta}\,\mathbf{A}^{\hat{\alpha}_{i}\![\delta]}{}_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}-\sum_{j=1}^{\lambda}\Gamma^{\delta}_{\gamma\beta_{j}}\,\mathbf{A}^{\bar{\alpha}}{}_{\hat{\beta}_{j}\![\delta]}{}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\bar{\Gamma}^{a_{i}}_{\gamma d}\,\mathbf{A}^{\bar{\alpha}}{}_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\hat{a}_{i}\![d]}{}_{\bar{b}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\bar{\Gamma}^{d}_{\gamma b_{j}}\,\mathbf{A}^{\bar{\alpha}}{}_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\hat{b}_{j}\![d]}\text{,}

where the Christoffel symbols

(A.2)

\nabla_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}:=\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\gamma}\text{,}\qquad\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\alpha}\partial_{b}:=\bar{\Gamma}^{c}_{\alpha b}\partial_{c}\text{,}

are given by

(A.3)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\rho\rho}^{\alpha}=-\rho^{-1}\delta^{\alpha}{}_{\rho}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\Gamma_{a\rho}^{\rho}=0\text{,}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{a\rho}^{c}=-\rho^{-1}\delta^{c}{}_{a}+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{cd}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{ad}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\Gamma_{\rho a}^{c}-\bar{\Gamma}_{\rho a}^{c}=-\rho^{-1}\delta^{c}{}_{a}\text{,}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{ab}^{\rho}=\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{ab}-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{ab}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\Gamma_{ab}^{c}-\bar{\Gamma}_{ab}^{c}=0\text{.}

A.1.
Proposition
3.7

Suppose that
\mathsf{A}
has rank
(k,l)
. (
3.2
) gives, with respect to any coordinates
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
,

(A.4)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{cd}\,\mathsf{D}_{e}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{g}^{a_{i}d}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{de}\,\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\hat{a}_{i}\![e]}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{b_{j}d}\,\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\hat{b}_{j}\![e]}\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}_{c}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{c}(\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{D}_{c}(\mathsf{g}^{a_{i}d}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{de}\,\mathsf{A}^{\hat{a}_{i}\![e]}{}_{\bar{b}})-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathsf{D}_{c}(\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{b_{j}d}\,\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\hat{b}_{j}\![e]})\text{.}

The difference of these equations reads

(A.5)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{c}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}+\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}-\mathsf{D}_{c}(\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{cd}\,\mathsf{D}_{e}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{g}^{a_{i}d}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{de}\,\mathsf{A}^{\hat{a}_{i}\![e]}{}_{\bar{b}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{b_{j}d}\,\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\hat{b}_{j}\![e]}\text{.}

We apply the commutation formula contained in
[
Sha20
, Proposition 2.27]
to obtain

(A.6)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{c}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{cd}\,\mathsf{D}_{e}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{g}^{a_{i}d}(\mathsf{D}_{e}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{cd}-\mathsf{D}_{d}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{ce})\mathsf{A}^{\hat{a}_{i}\![e]}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathsf{g}^{de}(\mathsf{D}_{b_{j}}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{cd}-\mathsf{D}_{d}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{cb_{j}})\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\hat{b}_{j}\![e]}\text{.}

Combining (
A.6
) with (
3.11
) yields the identity (
3.12
), as required.

Next, recalling Definition
3.5
of
\bar{\Box}
, we expand (partially in
\varphi_{\rho}
-coordinates)

(A.7)

\displaystyle\bar{\Box}(\rho^{p}\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\bar{\nabla}_{\alpha}(\rho^{p}\bar{\nabla}_{\beta}\mathsf{A}+p\rho^{p-1}\bar{\nabla}_{\beta}\rho\cdot\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle=\rho^{p}\bar{\Box}\mathsf{A}+2p\rho^{p-1}g^{\alpha\beta}\nabla_{\alpha}\rho\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\beta}\mathsf{A}+p(p-1)\rho^{p-2}g^{\alpha\beta}\nabla_{\alpha}\rho\nabla_{\beta}\rho\,\mathsf{A}+p\rho^{p-1}\Box\rho\,\mathsf{A}

\displaystyle=\rho^{p}\bar{\Box}\mathsf{A}+2p\rho^{p+1}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}+p(p-1)\rho^{p}\,\mathsf{A}+p\rho^{p-1}\Box\rho\,\mathsf{A}\text{.}

The formulae in (
A.3
) imply that

(A.8)

\displaystyle\Box\rho

\displaystyle=-\rho^{2}\Gamma^{\rho}_{\rho\rho}-\rho^{2}\mathsf{g}^{ab}\Gamma^{\rho}_{ab}

\displaystyle=-(n-1)\rho+\frac{1}{2}\rho^{2}\mathsf{g}^{ab}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{ab}\text{.}

Substituting (
A.8
) into (
A.7
) yields

(A.9)

\bar{\Box}(\rho^{p}\mathsf{A})=\rho^{p}\bar{\Box}\mathsf{A}+2p\rho^{p+1}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}-p(n-p)\rho^{p}\,\mathsf{A}+\frac{1}{2}p\rho^{p+1}\mathsf{g}^{ab}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{ab}\,\mathsf{A}\text{.}

Combining (
A.9
) with (
3.11
) immediately implies (
3.13
), as required.

∎

A.2.
Proposition
3.9

Assume all indices are with respect to
\varphi
- and
\varphi_{\rho}
-coordinates and let
\Gamma
and
\bar{\Gamma}
be the corresponding Christoffel symbols defined (
A.3
).
For future convenience, we set
l:=r_{1}+r_{2}
and define (via local coordinates) the vertical tensor fields

(A.10)

\mathsf{k}_{ac}:=\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{ac}-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{ac}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{k}^{b}{}_{a}:=\rho^{-1}\delta^{b}{}_{a}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{bc}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{ac}\text{.}

By the definitions of
\nabla
and
\bar{\mathsf{D}}
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\rho}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}(A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}})-\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\Gamma^{\rho}_{\rho\rho}\,A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\Gamma^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}\,A_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}(\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}})-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\bar{\Gamma}^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}\mathsf{A}_{\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}\text{.}

Subtracting these equations and applying (
3.14
) and (
A.3
) yields

\displaystyle\nabla_{\rho}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}-\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\Gamma^{\rho}_{\rho\rho}\,\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}(\Gamma^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}-\bar{\Gamma}^{b}_{\rho a_{j}})\mathsf{A}_{\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}+r_{1}\rho^{-1}\,\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}+r_{2}\rho^{-1}\,\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}+(r_{1}+r_{2})\rho^{-1}\,\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}\text{,}

from which (
3.20
) follows.

Similarly, the definitions of
\nabla
and
\bar{\mathsf{D}}
imply

\displaystyle\nabla_{c}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{c}(A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}})-\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\Gamma_{c\rho}^{b}\,A_{\hat{\rho}_{i}\![b]\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\Gamma_{ca_{j}}^{\beta}\,A_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![\beta]}\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{c}(\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}})-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\bar{\Gamma}_{ca_{j}}^{b}\,\mathsf{A}_{\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}\text{.}

Subtracting the above equations and recalling (
3.15
), (
3.16
), and (
A.3
), we obtain

(A.11)

\displaystyle\nabla_{c}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}-\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\Gamma_{c\rho}^{b}\,A_{\hat{\rho}_{i}\![b]\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\Gamma_{ca_{j}}^{\rho}\,A_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![\rho]}

(A.12)

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}+\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\mathsf{k}^{b}{}_{c}\,(\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{b\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{ca_{j}}\,(\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}\text{.}

Combining the above with (
3.11
) and (
A.10
) yields (
3.21
).

For (
3.22
), we start by computing
\rho
-derivatives.
By (
A.1
),

(A.13)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\rho\rho}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}(\nabla_{\rho}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}})-\Gamma^{\rho}_{\rho\rho}\,\nabla_{\rho}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\Gamma^{\rho}_{\rho\rho}\,\nabla_{\rho}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\Gamma^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}\,\nabla_{\rho}A_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}\text{,}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}(\nabla_{\rho}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}})+(r_{1}+1)\rho^{-1}\,\nabla_{\rho}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\Gamma^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}\,\nabla_{\rho}A_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}\text{,}

Similarly, for the corresponding mixed derivatives, we apply (
A.1
) and compute

(A.14)

\displaystyle\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{\rho\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-l}\partial_{\rho}[\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}]-\Gamma^{\rho}_{\rho\rho}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\bar{\Gamma}^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}[\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}]+(l+1)\rho^{-1}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\bar{\Gamma}^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}\text{,}

where we also applied (
A.3
) and the properties contained in Proposition
3.4
.
Subtracting (
A.14
) from (
A.13
), while applying both (
A.3
) and (
3.20
), we obtain that

(A.15)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\rho\rho}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{\rho\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}+(r_{1}-l)\rho^{-1}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}(\Gamma^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}-\bar{\Gamma}^{b}_{\rho a_{j}})\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{\rho\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}+(r_{1}-l)\rho^{-1}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}-r_{2}\rho^{-1}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{\rho\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}\text{.}

Next, we apply (
A.1
) to compute

\displaystyle\nabla_{bc}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{b}(\nabla_{c}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}})-\Gamma^{\alpha}_{bc}\,\nabla_{\alpha}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\Gamma^{d}_{b\rho}\,\nabla_{c}A_{\hat{\rho}_{i}\![d]\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\Gamma^{\delta}_{ba_{j}}\,\nabla_{c}A_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![\delta]}\text{,}

\displaystyle\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{bc}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{b}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}})-\Gamma^{\alpha}_{bc}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\alpha}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\bar{\Gamma}^{d}_{ba_{j}}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{A}_{\hat{a}_{j}\![d]}\text{.}

Subtracting the two equations and recalling (
A.3
) yields

(A.16)

\displaystyle\nabla_{bc}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{bc}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}+\partial_{b}(\nabla_{c}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}})-\Gamma^{\alpha}_{bc}[\nabla_{\alpha}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\alpha}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}]

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\Gamma^{d}_{b\rho}\,\nabla_{c}A_{\hat{\rho}_{i}\![d]\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\Gamma^{\rho}_{ba_{j}}\,\nabla_{c}A_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![\rho]}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\bar{\Gamma}^{d}_{ba_{j}}\,(\nabla_{c}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}})

\displaystyle:=\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{bc}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}+I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}+I_{5}\text{.}

To simplify the upcoming computations, we define, for all
1\leq i\leq r_{1}
and
1\leq j\leq r_{2}
, the vertical tensor fields
\mathsf{z}
,
\mathsf{z}^{\rho}_{i}
,
\mathsf{z}^{v}_{j}
—of ranks
(0,r_{2}+1)
,
(0,r_{2}+2)
,
(0,r_{2})
, respectively—via the index formulae

(A.17)

\displaystyle\mathsf{z}_{c\bar{a}}

\displaystyle:=\nabla_{c}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}\text{,}

\displaystyle(\mathsf{z}^{\rho}_{i})_{cb\bar{a}}

\displaystyle:=\nabla_{c}A_{\hat{\rho}_{i}\![b]\bar{a}}-\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{b\bar{a}}\text{,}

\displaystyle(\mathsf{z}^{v}_{j})_{c\hat{a}_{j}}

\displaystyle:=\nabla_{c}A_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![\rho]}-\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}\text{.}

Applying (
3.20
), (
A.3
), and (
A.17
) to the term
I_{2}
from (
A.16
), we obtain

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{k}_{bc}[\nabla_{\rho}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}]-\Gamma^{d}_{bc}(\nabla_{d}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{d}\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}})

\displaystyle=-\Gamma^{d}_{bc}(\nabla_{d}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{d}\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}})\text{.}

From (
A.16
), the first part of (
A.17
), and the above, we see that

(A.18)

I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{5}=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}\mathsf{z}_{c\bar{a}}\text{.}

Similarly, for
I_{3}
and
I_{4}
, we again apply (
A.3
) and (
A.17
):

(A.19)

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\mathsf{k}^{d}{}_{b}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{d\bar{a}}+\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\mathsf{k}^{d}{}_{b}\,(\mathsf{z}^{\rho}_{i})_{cd\bar{a}}\text{,}

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}b}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}b}\,(\mathsf{z}^{v}_{j})_{c\hat{a}_{j}}\text{.}

Now, recalling (
3.21
), along with (
A.17
), we deduce

(A.20)

\displaystyle\mathsf{z}_{c\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,(\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{e\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}c}\,(\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}\text{,}

\displaystyle(\mathsf{z}^{\rho}_{i})_{cd\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq r_{1}\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,(\mathsf{A}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{ed\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}c}\,(\mathsf{A}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{d\hat{a}_{j}}-\mathsf{k}_{bc}\,\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}\text{,}

\displaystyle(\mathsf{z}^{v}_{j})_{c\hat{a}_{j}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,(\mathsf{A}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{e\hat{a}_{j}}+\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,\mathsf{A}_{\hat{a}_{j}\![e]}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq r_{2}\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{i}c}\,(\mathsf{A}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\hat{a}_{i,j}}\text{.}

Combining (
3.11
), (
A.10
), (
A.18
), and the above, we conclude that

(A.21)

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{5}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}[\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}(\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{e\bar{a}}+\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}\mathsf{k}^{d}{}_{c}\,(\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{e\bar{a}}]-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}[\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}c}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}(\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}+\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}c}\,(\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}]

\displaystyle=\rho^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}(\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{c\bar{a}}-\rho^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{j}c}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}(\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}+\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\bar{\mathsf{D}}\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{cb\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\bar{\mathsf{D}}\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{cb\bar{a}}+\sum_{i=1}^{r_{2}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{cb\bar{a}}+\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{cb\bar{a}}\text{.}

Similar computations using (
A.19
) yield

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\mathsf{k}^{d}{}_{b}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{d\bar{a}}+2\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq r_{1}}\mathsf{k}^{d}{}_{b}\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,(\mathsf{A}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{ed\bar{a}}-\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{k}^{d}{}_{b}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}c}\,(\mathsf{A}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{d\hat{a}_{j}}-r_{1}\mathsf{k}^{d}{}_{b}\mathsf{k}_{dc}\,\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{b\bar{a}}-r_{1}\rho^{-2}\mathsf{g}_{bc}\,\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}+2\rho^{-2}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq r_{1}}(\mathsf{A}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}-\rho^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{j}c}\,(\mathsf{A}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{b\hat{a}_{j}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\bar{\mathsf{D}}\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{cb\bar{a}}+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{A})_{cb\bar{a}}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq r_{1}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{A}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\rho^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{A}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{cb\hat{a}_{j}}\text{,}

and

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}b}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}-\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}b}\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,(\mathsf{A}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{e\hat{a}_{j}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}b}\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,\mathsf{A}_{\hat{a}_{j}\![e]}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq r_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{i}c}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}b}\,(\mathsf{A}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\hat{a}_{i,j}}

\displaystyle=-\rho^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{j}b}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}-\rho^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{j}b}\,(\mathsf{A}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{c\hat{a}_{j}}-\rho^{-2}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{j}b}\,\mathsf{A}_{\hat{a}_{j}\![c]}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\rho^{-2}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq r_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{i}c}\mathsf{g}_{a_{j}b}\,(\mathsf{A}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\hat{a}_{i,j}}+\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{D}\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{cb\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{A}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{A})_{cb\bar{a}}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq r_{2}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{A}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}\text{.}

Finally, combining (
A.16
), (
A.21
), and the above, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{g}^{bc}\,\nabla_{bc}A_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-l}\mathsf{g}^{bc}\bar{\nabla}_{bc}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}+2\rho^{-1}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\mathsf{g}^{bc}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}(\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{c\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{a_{j}}(\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}-(nr_{1}+r_{2})\rho^{-2}\,\mathsf{A}_{\bar{a}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+2\rho^{-2}\left[\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq r_{1}}\mathsf{g}^{bc}\,(\mathsf{A}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}-\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}(\mathsf{A}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{a_{j}\hat{a}_{j}}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq r_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{i}a_{j}}\,(\mathsf{A}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\hat{a}_{i,j}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\bar{\mathsf{D}}\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{\bar{a}}+\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\bar{\mathsf{D}}\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{\bar{a}}+\sum_{i=1}^{r_{2}}\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{A}^{\rho}_{i})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{A}^{v}_{j})_{\bar{a}}+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{A})_{\bar{a}}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq r_{1}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{A}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{2}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{A}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq r_{2}}\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{A}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}\text{.}

(
3.22
) now follows from (
A.15
), the above, and the fact that

\Box A=\rho^{2}(\nabla_{\rho\rho}A+\mathsf{g}^{bc}\nabla_{bc}A)\text{,}\qquad\rho^{-1}\bar{\Box}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})=\rho^{2}[\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{\rho\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})+\mathsf{g}^{bc}\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{bc}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{A})]\text{.}

Appendix B
\mathsf{Q}^{0}
,
\mathsf{Q}^{1}
and
\mathsf{Q}^{2}

In this section we derive the precise forms of the vertical tensor fields
\mathsf{Q}^{0}
,
\mathsf{Q}^{1}
,
\mathsf{Q}^{2}
appearing in Proposition
4.15
. Let
Q_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}
denote the right-hand side of (
4.8
):

(B.1)

\displaystyle Q_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}:

\displaystyle=4\mathchoice{W^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.09462pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.09462pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.9pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49997pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{[\delta|}}}}{W^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.09462pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.09462pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.9pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49997pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{[\delta|}}}}{W^{{{\lambda}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.09462pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.09462pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.9pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49997pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.8583 {|\gamma]}}}}-\mathchoice{W^{{{\lambda}{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.63474pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.63474pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17776pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55554pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}{\delta}}}}{W^{{{\lambda}{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.63474pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.63474pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17776pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55554pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma}{\delta}}}}{W^{{{\lambda}{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.63474pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.63474pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17776pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55554pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.858 $}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha}{\beta}{\lambda}{\mu}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha}{\beta}{\lambda}{\mu}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\alpha}{\beta}{\lambda}{\mu}}}}

\displaystyle=g^{\lambda\nu}g^{\mu\sigma}\left(4W_{\nu\alpha\sigma[\delta|}W_{\lambda\beta\mu|\gamma]}-W_{\nu\sigma\gamma\delta}W_{\alpha\beta\lambda\mu}\right)

\displaystyle=\rho^{4}\left(4W_{\rho\alpha\rho[\delta|}W_{\rho\beta\rho|\gamma]}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{4}\mathsf{g}^{ef}\left(4W_{\rho\alpha f[\delta|}W_{\rho\beta e|\gamma]}-W_{\rho f\gamma\delta}W_{\alpha\beta\rho e}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{4}\mathsf{g}^{ef}\left(4W_{f\alpha\rho[\delta|}W_{e\beta\rho|\gamma]}-W_{f\rho\gamma\delta}W_{\alpha\beta e\rho}\right)

(B.2)

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{4}\mathsf{g}^{ef}\mathsf{g}^{gh}\left(4W_{f\alpha h[\delta|}W_{e\beta g|\gamma]}-W_{fh\gamma\delta}W_{\alpha\beta eg}\right)\text{,}

where we have applied the Fefferman-Graham gauge condition (
1.6
).
\mathsf{Q}^{0}
,
\mathsf{Q}^{1}
and
\mathsf{Q}^{2}
are given by

(B.3)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{abcd}=Q_{abcd}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{Q}^{1}_{bcd}=Q_{\rho bcd}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{Q}^{2}_{bd}=Q_{\rho b\rho d}\text{.}

In particular, one finds for
\mathsf{Q}^{0}
:

(B.4)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Q}^{0}_{abcd}

\displaystyle=4\mathsf{W}^{2}_{a[d}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{c]b}+2\mathsf{g}^{ef}\left[2\mathsf{W}^{1}_{[d|fa}\mathsf{W}^{1}_{|c]eb}+2\mathsf{W}^{1}_{af[d|}\mathsf{W}^{1}_{be|c]}-\mathsf{W}^{1}_{fcd}\mathsf{W}^{1}_{eab}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathsf{g}^{ef}\mathsf{g}^{gh}\bigg[2\left(\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}_{fahd}-\frac{2}{n-2}\left(\mathsf{g}_{f[h}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{d]a}+\mathsf{g}_{a[d}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{h]f}\right)\right)\left(\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}_{ebgc}+\frac{2}{n-2}\left(\mathsf{g}_{e[g}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{c]b}+\mathsf{g}_{b[c}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{g]e}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-2\left(\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}_{fahc}-\frac{2}{n-2}\left(\mathsf{g}_{f[h}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{c]a}+\mathsf{g}_{a[c}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{h]f}\right)\right)\left(\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}_{ebgd}+\frac{2}{n-2}\left(\mathsf{g}_{e[g}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{d]b}+\mathsf{g}_{b[d}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{g]e}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\left(\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}_{fhcd}-\frac{2}{n-2}\left(\mathsf{g}_{f[c}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{d]h}+\mathsf{g}_{h[d}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{c]f}\right)\right)\left(\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}_{abeg}+\frac{2}{n-2}\left(\mathsf{g}_{a[e}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{g]b}+\mathsf{g}_{b[g}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{e]a}\right)\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-2}\left(1;\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}\right)_{abcd}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-3}\left(\rho;\mathsf{W}^{1}\right)_{abcd}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-2}\left(1;\mathsf{W}^{2}\right)_{abcd}\text{,}

for
\mathsf{Q}^{1}
:

(B.5)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Q}^{1}_{bcd}

\displaystyle=-2\mathsf{g}^{ef}\left[\mathsf{W}^{1}_{fcd}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{be}+2\mathsf{W}^{2}_{f[d}\mathsf{W}^{1}_{c]eb}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathsf{g}^{ef}\mathsf{g}^{gh}\bigg[2\mathsf{W}^{1}_{fhd}\left(\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}_{ebgc}-\frac{2}{n-2}\left(\mathsf{g}_{e[g}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{c]b}+\mathsf{g}_{b[c}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{g]e}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-2\mathsf{W}^{1}_{fhc}\left(\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}_{ebgd}-\frac{2}{n-2}\left(\mathsf{g}_{e[g}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{d]b}+\mathsf{g}_{b[d}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{g]e}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left(\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}_{fhcd}-\frac{2}{n-2}\left(\mathsf{g}_{f[c}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{d]h}+\mathsf{g}_{h[d}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{c]f}\right)\right)\mathsf{W}^{1}_{beg}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-3}\left(\rho;\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}\right)_{bcd}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-2}\left(1;\mathsf{W}^{1}\right)_{bcd}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-3}\left(\rho;\mathsf{W}^{2}\right)_{bcd}\text{,}

and, finally, for
\mathsf{Q}^{2}
:

(B.6)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Q}^{2}_{bd}

\displaystyle=-2\mathsf{g}^{ef}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{fd}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{be}+\mathsf{g}^{ef}\mathsf{g}^{gh}\bigg[2\mathsf{W}^{1}_{fhd}\mathsf{W}^{1}_{geb}-\mathsf{W}^{1}_{dfh}\mathsf{W}^{1}_{beg}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\mathsf{W}^{2}_{fh}\left(\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}_{ebgd}-\frac{2}{n-2}\left(\mathsf{g}_{e[g}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{d]b}+\mathsf{g}_{b[d}\mathsf{W}^{2}_{g]e}\right)\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-2}\left(1;\hat{\mathsf{W}}^{0}\right)_{bd}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-3}\left(\rho;\mathsf{W}^{1}\right)_{bd}+\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}-2}\left(1;\mathsf{W}^{2}\right)_{bd}\text{,}

in which we have applied (
4.28
), (
4.29
) and (
4.30
).
